# Villager News — Dependency closure

Companion to [the audit](REPORT.md). This is a scoped static resolver, not a runtime loader. All entity/attachable animation/geometry/material/texture aliases, animate-list bindings, render-controller references, material inheritance, texture-set images and explicit loot/trade paths are below. Array and conditional render-controller payloads, transitions, bone tracks, function bodies and computed script identifiers remain exact in inventory.json; resolve those in their owning actor context, not globally. A declared definition does not prove Crossrock execution. `external/missing` requires investigation rather than an automatic fallback.

## Closure boundaries

- No direct entity/attachable animation, geometry or render-controller definition edge is unresolved in this archive scan. This does not validate controller-array evaluation or script-computed playback names.
- Vanilla/external materials and tables need Bedrock-equivalent implementations or verified Java mappings. They are transitive dependencies, not missing archive files.
- Conditional expressions and actor-local alias arrays are retained in the full definition bodies. Global string matching cannot prove their resolution for every state.
- Script constants, template literals and argument expressions are preserved in script.calls; all named handbook triggers/groups/variants are indexed separately. The dynamic endpoint space is not statically enumerated as fabricated constants.
- Clip-to-bone, render-array and texture paths are evaluated under the owning geometry/actor. The inventory retains every bone/keyframe/locator/array payload; runtime state exploration and Bedrock comparison remain acceptance work.
